Blind prompt-identification benchmark

Do descriptive words leave a visible trace?

This benchmark tests whether a blind judge can recover a descriptive word from model output. It measures observable behavior. It cannot establish that a model has a specific hidden definition of "lovely," "beautiful," or any other word.

Generated September 1, 2026Generator and judge: GPT-5.6 Terra5 runs per condition210 outputs in this report

95% interval19.4% to 32.0%Wilson interval
Writing outputs179Poems and paragraphs

The writing classifier scored above chance. This statement applies to the pooled writing runs. The row-level table below shows where the signal came from.

Everyday writing examples

These are first runs, chosen by a fixed rule before reading their content. Open all runs later in the report to compare every poem and paragraph.

Poem · Positive

Beautiful

Autumn Return

The road unspools beneath a copper sky,
and maples kindle softly by the lane.
My window gathers dusk; the swallows fly
like loosened stitches through the evening rain.
The porch light blooms before I reach the gate,
a small, warm star against the cooling blue.
The door remembers how to ease its weight;
the hall holds cedar, bread, and something true.
Inside, the kettle starts its patient song,
while wind combs gold from every waiting tree.
I set my travel-worn heart down where it belongs,
and autumn closes gently over me.

Blind prediction: Control

Poem · Positive

Lovely

Homeward at Dusk

Maples burn softly along the lane,
Rain-bright windows gather the gold.
My footsteps loosen the day’s old chain,
While chimney smoke curls blue in the cold.

A blackbird settles beneath the eaves,
The gate knows well the touch of my hand.
Warm light trembles through russet leaves,
And supper’s fragrance crosses the land.

Inside, the clock keeps its patient tune,
Coats bloom on hooks by the polished door.
Night rises gently, a harvest moon,
And home becomes my whole world once more.

Blind prediction: Control

Poem · Neutral

Ordinary

Autumn Return

The bus sighs softly at the corner curb,
Leaves follow me along the fading street.
A chimney sends its ribbon through the dusk,
And porch lights make the quiet pavement sweet.

My key is cold between my waiting hands,
The gate gives way with one familiar sound.
Inside, the hall holds warmth and lamplight still,
While rain begins to darken all the ground.

I hang my coat beside the kitchen door,
And hear the kettle gathering its song.
The evening settles gently in the rooms,
And home receives me like I belong.

Blind prediction: Control

Poem · Neutral

Plain

Autumn Return

The bus sighs low beside the amber street,
Leaves gather softly at the garden gate.
I carry dusk beneath my tired feet,
And smell the woodsmoke drifting warm and late.

The windows hold their squares of golden light,
A kettle hums behind the kitchen door.
My coat lets fall one copper leaf at night,
The familiar floor receives me once more.

Outside, the wind turns branches into rain,
Inside, the clock resumes its gentle chime.
I set my keys down, breathe, and feel again
The house make room for all the coming time.

Blind prediction: Control

Poem · Negative

Ugly

Porch Light, Wet Boots

Leaves stick blackly to my shoes.
The bus coughs away without me.
Rain has wrung the daylight thin.
I drag my coat through the gate.
The fence leans like a bad tooth.
Inside, the radiator clanks and spits.
Soup skin shivers in its bowl.
My keys scrape the wrong lock twice.
The hallway smells of damp wool.
The clock chews loudly at the wall.
I drop my bag beside the door.
Home opens its tired mouth and keeps me.

Blind prediction: Ugly

Poem · Negative

Unattractive

Homeward, With Wet Shoes
The bus coughs me onto a ditch of leaves.
Streetlamps buzz like tired appliances.
My key is cold and shaped like an excuse.
Rain has pasted receipts along the curb.
The porch sags under its bucket of dark.
Inside, the radiator clicks without warmth.
I drop my bag beside yesterday's mail.
The hallway smells of wool and old onions.
One brown leaf rides in on my heel.
The window shows a sky the color of dishwater.
I lock the door; it fails to improve anything.
Autumn keeps dripping from my coat.

Blind prediction: Unpleasant

Paragraph · Positive

Beautiful

Before dawn, the neighborhood bakery waits in a hush scented with butter and yeast. Inside, lamps spill amber pools across the tiled floor, where flour dust rests like pale snow beside the mixers. Rows of empty shelves seem to hold their breath, ready for loaves with crackling crusts, jam-bright pastries, and warm cinnamon rolls curled beneath their glaze. In the back, a baker folds dough with slow, practiced hands while the oven gathers its first gentle heat. Beyond the fogged front window, the street is still blue with morning, its parked cars beaded with dew and its sidewalks nearly empty. Soon the bell above the door will ring, coffee will murmur into cups, and familiar faces will arrive wrapped in scarves and sleepy conversation. For now, the bakery belongs to its quiet rituals: the rustle of paper bags, the soft thump of dough, and the promise rising invisibly in the air.

Blind prediction: Lovely

Paragraph · Positive

Lovely

Before dawn has fully lifted from the row of brick storefronts, the bakery is already awake behind its fogged windows. Warm light spills across the tiled floor as trays of croissants rise in neat, buttery crescents and loaves cool on wooden racks, their crusts singing softly in the hush. The air carries cinnamon, toasted sesame, and the deep, comforting scent of fresh bread into the still-blue street. A chalkboard waits by the door with the day's offerings, while paper bags stand open like little sails beside the register. Outside, a cyclist slows to glance in, and a pair of sparrows hop along the curb. Soon the bell above the door will ring, neighbors will trade greetings over coffee, and the first warm loaf will leave under someone's arm, turning an ordinary morning into a small neighborhood ritual.

Blind prediction: Gorgeous

Paragraph · Neutral

Ordinary

Before sunrise, the neighborhood bakery is already awake behind its fogged front windows. A row of cooling loaves rests on metal racks, their crusts crackling softly in the warm air. The baker moves between the oven and the counter, arranging croissants in neat baskets and brushing flour from the worktable. Coffee begins to drip in a small machine near the register, filling the room with a dark, comforting smell that mingles with butter and yeast. Outside, delivery trucks pass on the damp street while the sign over the door remains unlit. A paper list of the day's specials is taped beside the entrance, and a stack of clean pastry boxes waits below it. Soon the lights will come on, the bell will ring, and the first customers will step inside.

Blind prediction: Control

Paragraph · Neutral

Plain

At the corner of Maple and Third, the bakery is waking before the street does. Inside the front windows, rows of glass jars catch the pale blue light, while a chalkboard menu waits beside the door with yesterday's flour smudged along its frame. The ovens breathe out a warm scent of butter, yeast, and cinnamon, drifting past stacked crates of milk and crates of oranges on the sidewalk. Behind the counter, paper bags stand open in a neat line, and tongs rest beside trays of croissants whose layers shine under the lamps. A baker carries a sheet pan from the kitchen, leaving tiny tracks of flour across the dark tile. The espresso machine gives a brief hiss, then settles. Outside, a delivery bicycle rattles by and a dog pauses at the doorway. The sign is still unlit, but the first loaves are cooling on racks, ready for the morning customers.

Blind prediction: Control

Paragraph · Negative

Ugly

At five forty, the neighborhood bakery looks half-awake and slightly miserable beneath its buzzing yellow sign. Rainwater has collected in the cracked curb outside, reflecting the stacked trays visible through the fogged front window. Inside, flour clings to the black rubber mats, the metal counters, and the baker's damp apron, while a sweet, burnt smell of yesterday's sugar hangs stubbornly over the warmer scent of fresh dough. Croissants sit crookedly on a rack, their butter leaking onto a sheet pan, and pale rolls crowd together in dented baskets near the register. The display case is still dark, except for one flickering bulb that makes the cakes look gray at the edges. A delivery truck coughs somewhere down the block. The door remains locked, but a woman with a grocery cart has already stopped outside, peering in as the first loaves begin to crackle in the oven.

Blind prediction: Unattractive

Paragraph · Negative

Unattractive

At 5:40, the bakery crouches beneath its faded awning, its front window filmed with last night’s grease and fingerprints. Inside, fluorescent tubes buzz over metal racks holding pale rolls that seem to have sweated through their paper trays. A mop bucket squats beside the counter, carrying a sour smell that blends with yeast, burnt coffee, and something sweet gone stale. The baker, in a creased apron, drags a tray of buns from the oven and leaves a dark smear on the handle. Outside, delivery crates block half the narrow pavement, while pigeons peck at flour dust caught in the gutter. The handwritten price cards lean crookedly behind the glass, and a clock above the till has stopped three minutes before six. Soon the shutters will rise, letting in commuters who need breakfast badly enough not to look too closely.

Blind prediction: Unsightly

Isolation audit

630 / 630distinct successful process IDs
630 / 630distinct successful thread IDs
630 / 630distinct successful workspace IDs
  • Every generation, classification, and profile call used a new OS process, SDK client, Codex thread, temporary workspace, and temporary Codex home.
  • The judge received the neutral task, one canonical artifact, and a randomized candidate order. It did not receive the true word, run number, experiment name, or another run.
  • The parent process joined true labels to judgments only during scoring.
  • 7 rejected or interrupted attempts remain in the exports. Every retry used a new process, thread, workspace, and home.
  • 1 output repeated its hidden word and was excluded before scoring.

import { useState } from "react";

type ToggleProps = {
  enabled: boolean;
  onChange: () => void;
};

function Toggle({ enabled, onChange }: ToggleProps) {
  return (
    <button
      className={`toggle ${enabled ? "is-on" : ""}`}
      type="button"
      aria-pressed={enabled}
      onClick={onChange}
    >
      <span />
    </button>
  );
}

Homeward

The train exhales beside the river road,
Low windows kindle one by one in gold.
Leaves gather softly at the garden gate,
And chimney smoke arrives before the late.
The hedges wear a russet, windblown crown,
While dusk unbuttons all the sleeping town.

My key remembers turning in the door;
Warm lamplight pools across the wooden floor.
The kettle hums, the hallway holds my name,
And rain taps gently on the darkened pane.

Outside, the branches bow and shadows roam;
Inside, the evening folds me into home.

Homeward at Dusk

The maples burn beside the quiet lane,
And cider-colored light fills every pane.
I leave the restless city’s silver hum,
While chimney smoke says softly, “You have come.”

Wet leaves gather under my unhurried feet,
The evening folds its gold around the street.
A lamp blooms warm behind the curtained glass,
As swallows stitch the dark above the grass.

The door knows well the turning of my key,
The kettle sings a small, familiar plea.
In autumn’s hush, the wandering world grows still—
I am returned, and all the rooms are filled.

Autumn Threshold

The train sighs softly at the edge of town,
And maple leaves turn copper in the rain.
I follow windowlight through streets gone brown,
Past chimney smoke and gardens hushed again.
The dusk has folded gold beneath its wing,
While sparrows stitch the dark from eave to eave.
My key is warm, a small and shining thing,
The porch lamp glows among the fallen leaves.
Inside, the kettle hums its welcome tune,
And woolen rooms hold daylight's fading fire.
I hang the evening by the crescent moon,
Then come home wholly, heart and coat and mire.

Homeward Through October

The last bus sighs beneath the amber trees,
and rain-dark pavement holds the fading sky.
I carry dusk like apples in my sleeves,
while chimney smoke unthreads the evening high.

At every gate, the wind remembers names;
the maples lay their small red hands on stone.
A kitchen window gathers gold from flames,
and makes the waiting street feel less alone.

My key turns softly in the weathered door;
warm bread and cedar meet me in the hall.
The world grows quiet on the rugs and floor,
and autumn folds its wings around us all.

Homeward in Autumn

The dusk folds amber through the thinning trees,
A chimney writes its welcome in the blue;
Dry leaves go whispering along my knees,
And every lane remembers what I knew.

The river holds one last unbroken gleam,
While windows kindle gold behind the rain;
The fields, half-mist, grow tender as a dream,
And crows draw darkened stitches through the plain.

At last the gate gives softly to my hand,
The porch light warms the gathering evening's hem;
I cross the threshold into lamp-lit land,
And autumn, breathing, settles over them.

Homeward in Amber

The road unspools beneath a russet sky,
Leaves gather gold along the quiet lane.
Woodsmoke lifts its small, familiar sigh,
And evening taps like rain against the pane.
The porch light blooms before the daylight goes,
A harbor bright beyond the darkening field.

I hang the wind beside the crescent moon,
And let the warm rooms take me out of time.

Homeward Embers

The maples kindle softly by the lane,
While dusk folds gold beneath a violet sky.
My shoes remember every shining drain,
And chimney smoke draws welcome from on high.

A kettle hums behind the window glass;
The porch light gathers moths like drifting snow.

Then turn the latch I have always known.

The evening settles, amber, at my door.
I set my wandering heart down once again,
And autumn finds me safely home once more.

Autumn’s Welcome

The amber dusk unbuttons from the sky,
And leaves drift softly past the garden gate.
My window kindles gold against the rye,
While chimney smoke curls warmly as I wait.
The path remembers every tired shoe,
Its puddles holding fragments of the moon.
A kettle sings the evening slowly through,
And cedar shadows gather in the room.
I hang the day beside the old brass key,
Then breathe the apple-scented, cooling air.
Home opens like a lantern just for me,
With firelight folding peace into each chair.

Homeward Ember

The road wears copper beneath my feet,
and maples kindle the cooling air.
Windows bloom with a softened gold
where evening gathers in every square.

My coat holds smoke and orchard wind,
the day’s last crow calls from a wire.
Beyond the gate, the porch lamp wakes,
a small, unshaken amber fire.

with cinnamon, rain, and woolen light.
The season folds its russet wings,
and home receives the falling night.

Autumn's Return

The last gold leaf turns softly in the lane,
Blue dusk gathers at the garden gate;
I carry woodsmoke home like a refrain,
While windows kindle, warm and small and late.

The porch light blooms against the cooling air,
And apples breathe their sweetness from the bowl;
A woolen hush waits patiently upstairs,
The house remembering the shape of soul.

Beyond the glass, the evening crows depart,
Their dark wings folding into amber foam;
I set my weary footsteps by the hearth,
And find the season waiting: welcome home.

Homeward at Dusk

The maples burn softly along the lane,
their copper leaves loosening into rain.
One window glows through the gathering blue,
a small, steadfast star I have traveled to.
The gate knows the weight of my lingering hand;
the porch boards answer, warm as remembered land.

Homeward, Under October

At dusk, the maples gather fire along the lane.
My footsteps stir a small republic of leaves.
The river keeps its copper counsel beyond the fields,
while chimney smoke unthreads the cooling blue.
A single window kindles at the hill's low shoulder,
gold as bread, and just as welcome from afar.
The gate remembers the pressure of my hand.
In the garden, asters bow beneath their silver breath.
Then the familiar door gives way to lamplight,
to apples, wool, and rain asleep upon the coat.
Outside, the dark folds up the wandering road;
inside, my name is waiting in the quiet.

The Road Knows My Name

Maples kindle softly along the lane,
and dusk folds blue around the fields.
My boots remember each familiar stone;
the old gate loosens its small, warm sigh.
Smoke rises from the chimney like a prayer
the wind has carried faithfully all day.

gold pooled against the gathering rain.

and every shadow learns the shape of welcome.
Autumn leaves its rustling at the threshold;
inside, my heart hangs up its coat.

The Way Back

At dusk, the maples lift their lanterned hands,
and cider-colored light pools on the lane.
My footsteps stir the hush of fallen leaves;
each small crackle knows and says my name.
The chimneys braid blue smoke into the cold,
while windows bloom like hearths along the hill.
I pass the gate where summer's roses slept
and feel the turning year grow kind and still.
Then home appears, its porch lamp held aloft,
a star made warm enough for human weather.
I cross the threshold, carrying the dusk,
and all the scattered parts of me come together.

The Road to Our Door

Smoke gathers low beneath the paling sky,

Lanterns at the Door

The train leaves its silver breath behind,
and maples stitch the dusk with fire.
My footsteps know the narrow lane
before my eyes can name the shire.
Smoke lifts softly from the chimneys,
warm spice gathers in the air.
The porch light blooms across the rain,
a small gold answer waiting there.
I turn the key; the old floor sings.
Coats, cups, and quiet hold me near.
Outside, the leaves keep falling down;
inside, I have arrived again.

Homeward

At dusk, I turn beneath the bronze-lit trees,
Cold air carries woodsmoke down the lane.
The last leaves gather softly at my knees,
And window lamps appear through misty rain.
My footsteps know the hush between the fields,
The gate's familiar creak, the garden wall.
The fading sky lays ember-colored shields
Across the roofs that wait beyond it all.
A kettle sings behind the curtained glass,
Warm gold spills gently over worn stone floors.
I leave the darkening road and let it pass,
Then breathe, at last, beside my open door.

Autumn Return

The bus sighs softly at the corner curb,
Leaves follow me along the fading street.
A chimney sends its ribbon through the dusk,
And porch lights make the quiet pavement sweet.

My key is cold between my waiting hands,
The gate gives way with one familiar sound.
Inside, the hall holds warmth and lamplight still,
While rain begins to darken all the ground.

I hang my coat beside the kitchen door,
And hear the kettle gathering its song.
The evening settles gently in the rooms,
And home receives me like I belong.

Autumn Return

The bus sighs low beside the amber square,
Leaves gather softly in the cooling air.
My keys are warm within my coat-lined hand,
The porch lamp pools its gold across the land.

A chimney threads a ribbon through the blue,
The windows hold a quiet, honeyed view.

Night settles gently over roof and tree,
And home opens its patient arms to me.

Autumn Return

The bus lets me off beneath a copper sky.
Leaves gather softly along the curb.
My keys are cool inside my hand.
Smoke rises from a neighbor's chimney.
The porch light warms the darkened steps.
I hear a dog bark once, then settle.
The garden bends beneath the evening dew.
An apple scent drifts from the kitchen.
My coat hangs heavy by the door.
The rooms remember the shape of me.
Outside, the wind moves through bare branches.
At last, I close the day behind me.

Autumn Return

The bus lets me off beneath a thinning sky,
Leaves gather softly at the garden gate.
My key turns warm and familiar in the lock,
While chimney smoke drifts low above the lane.
Inside, the hall holds yesterday's faint heat,
And boots leave small dark crescents on the mat.
A kettle wakes and murmurs by the stove,
The windows shine with amber from within.
I hang the evening on its wooden hook,
Then hear the rain begin against the glass.
The rooms receive me without asking why,
And home closes gently around my tired heart.

Homeward

The maples burn along the lane,
and dusk gathers at the gate.
My steps know every cracked stone
and the hush beside the hedge.

I leave the road behind me
and enter the house of my dreams.

Homeward at Dusk

The maples kindle softly by the lane,
And evening gathers blue along the hill.
I carry woodsmoke, rain, and train-song home,
While leaves turn slowly in the cooling air.
The porch light blooms before I reach the gate,
A small gold welcome through the window glass.
Inside, the kettle murmurs on the stove,
My coat falls warm against the hallway chair.
The rooms remember footsteps, laughter, names,
And supper's scent has settled into peace.
Outside, the wind combs bronze across the yard;
Within, I am returned to what I love.

Homeward in Autumn

The streetlamps kindle through the thinning rain,
And amber leaves collect along the lane.
My footsteps stir the dusk beneath the trees,
While chimney smoke goes wandering on the breeze.

The windows bloom with gold against the gray,
And supper scents drift softly down the way.
I lift the latch; the hallway holds its glow,
With coats and quiet waiting in a row.

Outside, the wind turns gardens dark and bare,
But warmth is folded gently in the air.
I close the door, and hear the kettle sing—
The night comes in, and home becomes a spring.

Homeward in Autumn

The dusk folds softly over amber trees,
My footsteps stir the leaves along the lane.
A chimney breathes its woodsmoke on the breeze,
And windowlight warms the coming rain.

The fields lie quiet under fading gold,
The crows fly home in ragged, calling lines.

And watch the evening gather at the pines.

At last, the porch lamp glows beside the door,
Its welcome spilling kindly on the stone.
I turn the key, hear firelight's gentle roar,
And autumn settles, softly, into home.

Homeward at Dusk

The maples burn beside the winding lane,
Their copper leaves rehearsing summer's loss.
I hear the evening train beyond the rain,
And see the fields grow dim beneath the frost.

My windows wait, two lamps against the blue,
While chimney smoke unthreads the cooling air.
The gate remembers every step I knew;
The porch light makes a small and faithful prayer.

Inside, the kettle starts its silver song,
The floorboards greet the weight of weary shoes.
I close the door; the dark can drift along—
Autumn has brought me back to what I choose.

At the Threshold

Porch Light, Like a Bad Tooth

The Door Sticks

The Rusted Threshold

I drag my boots through leaf-soup, brown and wet,
Past gutters coughing up a bitter tune.
The porch light blinks like an infected eye,
And moths collide beneath the swollen moon.
My key tastes iron in the lock's cold mouth;
The hallway breathes of cabbage, dust, and rain.

While orange shadows clot the windowpane.

Its sleeves still full of wind and chimney smoke.
Then home receives me, creaking at the seams,
A sagging jaw that opens for a ghost.

# Soot-Colored Doorstep

Homeward Through October

The dusk spat copper pennies at my shoes.
I dragged my coat through puddles thick as stew.
Bare trees scratched the sky with blackened forks.
A bus coughed smoke across the cabbage-dark.
My windows glowed like teeth in a wet mouth.

I dropped the day beside the peeling door.
Then autumn shut its muddy fist outside.
And home, at last, grew quiet round my bones.

The Door Swells Shut

Rain gnaws the last leaves into the gutter,
and every window wears a dead, yellow stare.

the lock coughs open on its rusted breath.

How to read this

  • Accuracy above chance means the modifier changed output in a way this judge could recognize.
  • Accuracy at chance does not prove that the words had no effect. The effect may be weak, inconsistent, or invisible to this judge.
  • Five runs per condition are enough to expose large differences, but one error moves condition recall by 20 percentage points.
  • The pooled writing test has more samples. It still combines tasks and word rows, so the row-level results matter.

Input exports: five-runs-per-condition-export.json, poem-positive-export.json, poem-neutral-export.json, poem-negative-export.json, paragraph-positive-export.json, paragraph-neutral-export.json, paragraph-negative-export.json

Official SDK reference: OpenAI Codex SDK documentation. The SDK starts a new thread with startThread(); this benchmark adds a fresh process, client, workspace, and temporary home for each call.
